KiCad board complexity scores
=============================
3146 boards from 819 open-source projects, sorted from most to least complex.
Each line gives the complexity score against the board's KiCad file name, plus the numbers behind it.

HOW THE SCORE IS CALCULATED (measured directly from each .kicad_pcb file)
  SCORE = TRACKS + 3 x VIAS + 2 x PARTS + 60 x BIG_ICS + 150 x BGAS
  Boards with fewer than 10 parts are scaled down in proportion, so a file with no components scores 0.
    PARTS    real components: footprints with 2 or more pads (stitching-via footprints, mounting
             holes, fiducials and logos are not counted)
    TRACKS   routed copper track segments and arcs
    VIAS     vias
    BIG_ICS  chips with 32+ pads in QFP/QFN/BGA/LGA/DIMM-style packages
    BGAS     BGA/LGA/CSP chips with 25+ pads (counted again on top of BIG_ICS)
    NETS     distinct nets (shown for information only, not in the score)
    LAYERS   copper layers
    KICAD    KiCad major version that wrote the file (the file opens in that version or newer)
  LEVEL: EXTREME >= 20000, VERY HIGH >= 8000, HIGH >= 3000, MEDIUM >= 1000, LOW >= 250, SIMPLE < 250

NOTES COLUMN
  panel            several copies of one board on a single sheet, which inflates every number
  mostly unrouted  many parts but few tracks (unfinished layout)
  no components    tracks only (for example imported gerbers or artwork)

READ THIS
  - A board with many parts but almost no routed tracks (a blank prototype plank, an unrouted
    layout) scores low on purpose: the score rates routing and chip complexity, not part count alone.
  - Identical files and forks/mirrors of the same board were removed. Revisions of one board
    (Rev A, Rev B...) are kept as separate entries.
  - ZIP PART says which zip file holds the board. The parts are ordered by score, so part01 has the
    most complex boards. PATH INSIDE ZIP is <project folder>/<path inside that project's repo>.
  - Every project folder also has its LICENSE and README (prefixed with _). Licences differ per
    project (CERN-OHL, GPL, MIT, Apache, CC...). Check them before reusing a design commercially.
    kicad_board_sources.txt lists every project with its web address.
  - Open a board in KiCad's PCB Editor with File > Open.
  - Only the .kicad_pcb files are included (no schematics), as asked.
